# Sprint brief: Tante Rosi — pregnancy coach bot

**Plan name:** `tante-rosi-pregnancy-bot`
**Profile:** `holmes-claude` with `--phase-model plan=claude:high` and `--robustness standard`
**Repo:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas`
**Base branch:** `main` (current HEAD: `8c5da74` "Add local relative time context")

## 1. Background and motivation

The Veas platform hosts multiple chatbot personas. Two ship today:
- **Véas** — dyad-shape mediator for romantic relationships (`bot_id='mediator'`, `participants_shape='dyad'`, `primary_topic_slug='relationship'`).
- **Coach** — solo-shape career coach (`bot_id='coach'`, `participants_shape='solo'`, `primary_topic_slug='career'`). Reference commit: `b0e0495`.

The architecture refactor across sprints S1–S6 made multi-bot ownership and per-bot tool allowlists first-class. The coach bot is the canonical solo exemplar. Adding a third solo bot now is the proof-of-architecture moment.

This sprint adds **Tante Rosi**, a solo-shape pregnancy coach. She supports a single pregnant user across the arc of pregnancy: knowing their current gestational week, surfacing upcoming appointments (via existing `scheduled_tasks`), tracking emotional/symptom themes (via existing `themes` and `observations`), and handling medical red flags via a strengthened `out_of_bounds` framework. The bot's voice is a warm, knowledgeable aunt ("Tante" = aunt in German) who knows when to defer to a clinician.

### Why this bot now

Three drivers:
1. **Real user need** — the principal user wants a bot that supports a real pregnancy in their life.
2. **Architecture validation** — the third bot proves the multi-bot architecture works without requiring foundational refactors.
3. **Medical-safety pattern** — pregnancy is the first health-adjacent bot; the OOB framework's medical-escalation pattern that lands here will be reusable for future health bots.

### Explicit non-goal: no generic abstraction

A prior design exploration (`docs/longitudinal-state.md`) proposed a generic `user_journeys` abstraction to accommodate pregnancy, fitness, weight, sobriety, etc. **That doc is rejected for this sprint** after review. Rationale: we have zero examples in production; the abstraction was designed against speculation about three hypothetical bots, not one real one. The right move is per-feature schema for Rosi, then extract a shared abstraction only after 3+ bots show genuine pattern overlap.

Keep `docs/longitudinal-state.md` as exploration reference. Add a header note: "Rejected 2026-05-12 in favor of per-feature schemas — see `docs/pregnancy-state.md`. Revisit if 3+ bots show pattern overlap." Do not implement against it.

## 2. Scope

### 2.1 In-scope deliverables

#### Schema

**Migration `migrations/0033_pregnancy.sql`** — additive, nullable, no defaults required:

```sql
BEGIN;

ALTER TABLE mediator.users
  ADD COLUMN pregnancy_edd date,
  ADD COLUMN pregnancy_dating_basis text
    CHECK (pregnancy_dating_basis IS NULL OR pregnancy_dating_basis IN ('lmp', 'scan')),
  ADD COLUMN pregnancy_lmp_date date,
  ADD COLUMN pregnancy_scan_date date,
  ADD COLUMN pregnancy_scan_corrected_at timestamptz,
  ADD COLUMN pregnancy_started_at timestamptz,
  ADD COLUMN pregnancy_ended_at timestamptz,
  ADD COLUMN pregnancy_outcome text
    CHECK (pregnancy_outcome IS NULL OR pregnancy_outcome IN ('birth', 'loss', 'termination')),
  ADD CONSTRAINT pregnancy_dating_basis_requires_edd
    CHECK ((pregnancy_edd IS NULL AND pregnancy_dating_basis IS NULL)
        OR (pregnancy_edd IS NOT NULL AND pregnancy_dating_basis IS NOT NULL)),
  ADD CONSTRAINT pregnancy_outcome_requires_ended_at
    CHECK ((pregnancy_outcome IS NULL AND pregnancy_ended_at IS NULL)
        OR (pregnancy_outcome IS NOT NULL AND pregnancy_ended_at IS NOT NULL));

-- Partial index for "find users with an active pregnancy" queries
CREATE INDEX idx_users_active_pregnancy
  ON mediator.users (id)
  WHERE pregnancy_edd IS NOT NULL AND pregnancy_ended_at IS NULL;

COMMIT;
```

**Migration `migrations/0034_pregnancy_topic.sql`**:

```sql
BEGIN;

INSERT INTO mediator.topics (id, slug, display_name)
VALUES (gen_random_uuid(), 'pregnancy', 'Pregnancy')
ON CONFLICT (slug) DO NOTHING;

COMMIT;
```

Migration safety notes:
- All new columns are nullable. No existing rows are affected; no backfill required.
- The two CHECK constraints prevent invalid partial-state. They are `NOT VALID`-safe even on large tables because new constraints only validate new/updated rows by default — but we accept full validation here because the table is small.
- Rollback: `0033_pregnancy.down.sql` drops columns and index; topic row deletion is omitted from `0034.down.sql` because deletion would fail if any user_bot_state row references it.

#### Helper module `app/services/pregnancy.py`

A small, pure module — no DB calls — exporting:

```python
from datetime import date, datetime, timezone
from typing import Literal

GESTATION_WEEKS = 40
LATE_OVERDUE_WEEKS = 42

def gestational_age(edd: date, today: date | None = None) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """Return (weeks, days) since LMP-equivalent (40w before EDD).

    Examples:
      EDD 2026-10-22, today 2026-05-12 → (17, 2)
    Clamped to [(0, 0), (LATE_OVERDUE_WEEKS, 6)]; raises if EDD is more
    than 1 year out (data error guard).
    """

def trimester(weeks: int) -> Literal['first', 'second', 'third']:
    """First: 0–12, Second: 13–27, Third: 28+. Standard clinical cut."""

def is_pregnancy_active(user) -> bool:
    """True iff edd is set AND ended_at is null."""

def days_since_loss(user, today: date | None = None) -> int | None:
    """For sensitivity rendering. Returns days since pregnancy_ended_at
    when outcome=loss, else None."""

def format_pregnancy_state(user) -> str | None:
    """Return the prompt-ready render block, or None if no pregnancy.

    Active form:
      ## Pregnancy
      - 17w2d (second trimester, EDD 2026-10-22, basis: lmp)
      - started: 2026-01-15

    Recent loss form (within 90 days of ended_at):
      ## Pregnancy
      - Recent loss (12 days ago). Handle with care.

    Recent birth form (within 90 days):
      ## Pregnancy (concluded)
      - Birth 12 days ago (EDD was 2026-10-22).

    Older than 90 days post-conclusion: returns None.
    """
```

**Edge cases the implementation must handle:**

- EDD set, gestational age computes to (-1, x) → clamp to (0, 0) and surface a warning in the render
- EDD set more than 1 year out → raise `ValueError` (data error; tool must reject)
- EDD passed by more than 2 weeks (overdue) → render `"42w (overdue, EDD was 2026-10-22)"`
- Twin pregnancies — no schema field for this in v1; mention in the persona as a follow-up if it comes up
- Pregnancy `started_at` may pre-date the conversation if Rosi onboards a user already pregnant

**Timezone:** EDD is stored as `date` (no timezone). `today` is computed in the user's `timezone` if set, falling back to UTC. The helper accepts an explicit `today` arg for testability.

#### Three write tools

In `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, following the existing tool registration pattern (matching e.g. `add_memory`, `update_oob`):

```python
async def set_pregnancy_edd(
    pool,
    ctx,
    *,
    edd: str,                            # ISO date
    dating_basis: Literal['lmp', 'scan'],
    lmp_date: str | None = None,
    scan_date: str | None = None,
    started_at: str | None = None,       # ISO datetime; defaults to now()
) -> dict:
    """Initial pregnancy capture. Errors if there is already an active
    pregnancy for ctx.user.id. Sets edd, dating_basis, optional provenance
    (lmp/scan dates), and pregnancy_started_at.
    """

async def correct_pregnancy_edd(
    pool,
    ctx,
    *,
    edd: str,
    dating_basis: Literal['lmp', 'scan'],
    scan_date: str | None = None,
) -> dict:
    """Mid-pregnancy correction (e.g. dating scan revises EDD). Requires
    an active pregnancy. Preserves pregnancy_started_at. Sets
    pregnancy_scan_corrected_at = now() when dating_basis flips to 'scan'.
    """

async def end_pregnancy(
    pool,
    ctx,
    *,
    outcome: Literal['birth', 'loss', 'termination'],
    ended_at: str | None = None,
) -> dict:
    """Close the active pregnancy. Errors if no active pregnancy.
    Records outcome + ended_at. Does NOT delete the historical columns
    so the bot can still render 'recent loss/birth' state for 90 days.
    """
```

Tool requirements:
- **Idempotency:** Each tool checks current state and errors clearly if preconditions fail. Re-running `end_pregnancy` after closure errors with `"pregnancy already ended on <date>"`.
- **Concurrency:** Wrap each in a single transaction with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the user row to prevent race conditions if two tool calls arrive simultaneously.
- **Tool-call logging:** Pregnancy tool calls land in the existing `turn_audit_events` table; no special logging.
- **No PII in tool result strings.** Return shape: `{"ok": true, "edd": "2026-10-22", "gestational_age": "17w2d"}`. Avoid echoing message content.
- **Tool descriptions** (the strings the LLM sees in `tool_schemas.py`): write these as if explaining to a thoughtful but cautious aunt. Specify which to call when. Cite the precondition errors explicitly so the LLM doesn't loop.

#### Tool allowlist

In `app/bots/registry.py`, register Tante Rosi (`bot_id='tante_rosi'`) modeled on the coach pattern but with pregnancy tools added:

```python
TANTE_ROSI_TOOL_EXCLUSIONS = COACH_TOOL_EXCLUSIONS | frozenset({
    # nothing extra excluded today
})

TANTE_ROSI_TOOL_ADDITIONS = frozenset({
    "set_pregnancy_edd",
    "correct_pregnancy_edd",
    "end_pregnancy",
})

_bot_specs["tante_rosi"] = BotSpec(
    bot_id="tante_rosi",
    prompt_renderer=_tante_rosi_prompt_renderer,
    step_instructions=_STEP_INSTRUCTIONS,
    display_name="Tante Rosi",
    participants_shape="solo",
    primary_topic_slug="pregnancy",
    read_scopes=ReadScopes(
        topics={"own"},
        allow_cross_topic_peek=True,
        allow_cross_topic_status_injection=False,
    ),
    write_scopes=WriteScopes(topics={"own"}),
    cross_topic_policy="peek",
    tool_allowlist=(frozenset(TOOL_DISPATCH.keys()) - TANTE_ROSI_TOOL_EXCLUSIONS)
                   | TANTE_ROSI_TOOL_ADDITIONS,
    bot_spec_version="1.0.0",
)
```

- **Coach allowlist must NOT include the pregnancy tools.** Add an explicit test that asserts `'set_pregnancy_edd' not in coach.tool_allowlist`.
- **Mediator allowlist must NOT include the pregnancy tools.** Same test for mediator.
- The pregnancy tools register in `TOOL_DISPATCH` but are gated by allowlist.

#### Hot context render

**Two render paths** must show pregnancy state:

**A) Solo path (`app/services/hot_context_solo.py`):** Tante Rosi's own prompt. Render the full block via `format_pregnancy_state(user)` near the top, after `topic_status` and before active themes. If `format_pregnancy_state` returns None, omit the section entirely.

**B) Dyad path (`app/services/hot_context.py`):** Véas (mediator) talking to one partner whose other partner is pregnant. Render a single, terse, hard-coded one-liner:

```
## Partner state
- <partner_name> is currently <Xw Yd> pregnant (EDD <date>).
```

This is the ONLY pregnancy data the mediator path surfaces. No symptom themes, no weight, no recent measurements. The mediator must never auto-bridge raw pregnancy content.

For a recent loss case (within 90 days), the mediator path renders:

```
## Partner state
- <partner_name>'s pregnancy ended recently (loss, <N> days ago). Handle with care.
```

Pregnancy state visibility from the mediator side is governed by **the partner's** pregnancy data, not the current user's. The render function takes both `user` and `partner` and decides which to show.

Both paths must:
- Tolerate `pregnancy_edd IS NULL` cleanly (omit the section)
- Tolerate ended pregnancies older than 90 days (omit the section)
- Tolerate `edd` set with `dating_basis = null` (data corruption — log a warning, omit the section, do not crash)

#### Persona file `app/bots/prompts/tante_rosi.py`

Create the directory `app/bots/prompts/` (does not exist yet — first bot to need it). Inside, `tante_rosi.py` exports `render_system_prompt(**kwargs) -> str`. Follow the coach exemplar pattern (delegate to a shared solo-prompt function and add bot-specific layering).

The persona body must include:

1. **Voice register** — warm aunt, addresses the user with care, uses "we" sparingly and never patronizingly. Calibration samples provided by user during Phase 2.
2. **Medical-defer guardrails** — explicit, frequent: "I am not a doctor. For anything that worries you, please call your midwife/OB." This phrasing or near-equivalent must appear when any symptom is described.
3. **Loss/grief stance** — when `pregnancy_outcome = 'loss'` or `'termination'` is recent, the persona MUST NOT default to celebratory framing about pregnancy. Specific guidance: do not ask about gestational week, do not surface unrelated bright cheer. Acknowledge the loss; offer presence; ask what kind of support is wanted.
4. **Complication handling** — high-risk pregnancy mentions (e.g. prior miscarriage, gestational diabetes, preeclampsia signs) should down-shift Rosi's tone to careful presence + escalation. Persona includes a small list of "elevate to OOB immediately" triggers.
5. **Onboarding stance** — if `pregnancy_edd IS NULL` and the user mentions pregnancy, Rosi's first job is to capture EDD or LMP. Persona includes a small script for this turn:
   - "Congratulations. To support you well, may I know roughly when you're due, or when your last period was?"
   - On answer: call `set_pregnancy_edd` with the appropriate `dating_basis`.
6. **Multilingual stance** — defaults to the user's incoming language; comfortable in German if the user uses it; uses "Tante" register either way.
7. **Boundaries** — refuses requests outside scope: financial advice, partner relationship advice (deflect to Véas), explicit medical diagnosis.

Persona file approximate length: 80–150 lines of prompt-text. The `_tante_rosi_prompt_renderer` function composes the persona + the standard solo prompt scaffolding (read/consult/respond/record/schedule steps).

#### OOB seed entries for medical red flags

The OOB framework currently surfaces user-reported sensitive content; we additionally need **pre-loaded medical red-flag patterns** that Rosi recognizes and escalates. Implementation choice (decide during phase 1):

**Option A:** Hard-code a list of patterns in `app/bots/prompts/tante_rosi.py` that the persona instructs the LLM to recognize. No DB rows; cheap; relies on LLM recognition.

**Option B:** Insert seed rows into `out_of_bounds` with `severity='hard'` and a special owner sentinel (e.g. a `pregnancy_red_flags` system pseudo-user). The persona references them by reading them in hot context. More structured; harder to ship; benefits from versioning.

**Recommended: Option A for v1.** Persona-side recognition is the more reliable path for medical red flags. The OOB *table* still receives user-specific entries for genuine OOB content (e.g. the user has high-risk pregnancy notes); the persona handles general red-flag recognition.

Specific red flags the persona must recognize and escalate (severity `hard` + clinical-deferral copy):

| Symptom | Escalation copy |
|---|---|
| Heavy vaginal bleeding | "Please call your midwife or go to the ER right now." |
| Severe persistent headache with vision changes or swelling | "This could be pre-eclampsia. Please call your clinic today, or go to the ER if it's getting worse fast." |
| Decreased fetal movement after week 24 | "Please call your midwife or labor & delivery now — they'll want to check." |
| Persistent severe vomiting / unable to keep fluids down | "This can become serious. Please call your clinic today." |
| Fever above 38.5°C / 101.3°F | "Please call your clinic today." |
| Sudden severe abdominal pain | "Please go to the ER." |
| Leaking fluid before 37 weeks | "Please call your midwife or labor & delivery now." |
| Thoughts of self-harm | Existing OOB hard-severity path; persona reinforces clinical referral. |

User will validate and possibly extend this list in Phase 2.

2. **BotSpec** — registered in `app/bots/registry.py` (above).

4. **Staging seed** — add a Tante Rosi test user to `app/staging.py` so replay tests exercise the new code paths.
5. **Prompt module** — `app/bots/prompts/tante_rosi.py` (above).
6. **Scheduled tasks** — no new types needed; existing `scheduled_tasks` handles appointment reminders.
7. **Evals** — `evals/per_bot/tante_rosi/.gitkeep` to scaffold; no scenarios filled out.
8. **Per-bot panels** — `scripts/check_per_bot_panels.py` must include `tante_rosi` after first deploy.

#### Tests

Add tests across the existing structure:

- `tests/test_pregnancy_helpers.py` — `gestational_age` at boundaries (0w, 13w/14w trimester transition, 27w/28w, 39w, 40w, 41w overdue, 42w late overdue, EDD in past by >2 weeks), `trimester`, `format_pregnancy_state` snapshots for all 5 render branches (no pregnancy, active early/mid/late, recent loss, recent birth, older than 90 days), `is_pregnancy_active`.
- `tests/test_pregnancy_tools.py` — happy path for all three tools; precondition errors (already-active on `set`, no-active on `correct`/`end`, invalid `dating_basis`, invalid `outcome`, EDD >1y out); concurrent set/end via simulated lock contention.
- `tests/test_pregnancy_allowlist.py` — Tante Rosi has the three tools; coach doesn't; mediator doesn't.

- `tests/test_pregnancy_persona.py` — load the persona, render a system prompt, assert key strings present (medical-defer phrasing, onboarding script when EDD null, loss-handling guidance when outcome=loss).
- `tests/test_pregnancy_oob.py` — feed Rosi a sample message containing each red-flag symptom in turn; assert the LLM trace would route to OOB escalation. (May require mocked LLM; align with how existing OOB tests mock.)

All existing tests must continue passing. The four known pre-existing failures from the audit (`test_agentic_e2e_ordering_cache_spend_and_oob`, 3× `test_decay.py::*`) are not blockers but should be noted in the final summary.

### 2.2 Out of scope

- Generic `user_journeys` / longitudinal-state abstraction
- Fitness, weight, sobriety, or any other coach-shape bot
- Pregnancy *history* (a `pregnancies` table for users who've been pregnant before) — when a user has a second pregnancy, the current columns are repurposed; if we want full history later, add a table then
- `user_pending_inputs` generalized acquisition mechanism — separate proposal, separate sprint
- Cross-bot pregnancy visibility beyond the single hard-coded mediator render line
- Postpartum bot — separate sprint, kicked off at birth time
- Numeric weight-gain tracking — Rosi may discuss weight in conversation and record as a memory/theme; no dedicated schema
- Symptom logging with structured severity — use existing `observations` and `themes`
- Automated week-by-week content delivery — Rosi answers on-demand; no scheduled "week 18 update" pushes in v1
- Wearable integration (sleep, HR, contractions) — far future
- Insurance/clinic coordination — not in scope ever

## 3. Phasing

This is **one sprint** but with a hard internal phase gate at code-complete.

### Phase 1 — Schema + plumbing (autonomous, ~3–4 days)

Megaplan executes the full phase 1 autonomously:

1. Branch + migrations 0033/0034 written
2. `app/services/pregnancy.py` written + tested
3. Three write tools written + tested + registered in `TOOL_DISPATCH`
4. BotSpec stub registered (with a placeholder persona — single line "Tante Rosi prompt (phase 2 content pending)")
5. Hot context render paths wired (both solo and dyad)
6. Allowlist tests pass
7. Migration tests pass
8. Full `pytest -q` at or above baseline (modulo 4 known pre-existing failures)
9. Commit + push to `rosi-pregnancy-bot`
10. **Gate to phase 2:** code-complete; bot is silent because persona content is placeholder.

### Phase 2 — Persona + medical content (user-collaborative, ~3–5 days)

Cannot proceed without user-provided inputs. Megaplan should pause and request:

- **Voice samples** — 5–10 example replies in the desired Tante Rosi tone, ideally across registers: warm greeting, sympathetic response to anxiety, gentle redirect to a clinician, response to a loss disclosure.
- **Red-flag list** — confirmation/edits to the medical escalation table above.
- **Multilingual stance** — exact instruction (default user language? German-comfortable? translate-on-request?).
- **Bot name** — confirm "Tante Rosi" or alternative.
- **Transport binding** — Discord channel ID, WhatsApp number, or other (must match an existing transport adapter).
- **Week-by-week content** *(optional)* — short blurbs per gestational week if user wants pre-canned material. If not provided, Rosi answers from the LLM's general knowledge with the medical-defer rails.

With inputs in hand:

1. Persona file finished
2. OOB red-flag recognition wired into persona prompt
3. Staging deployment: bot row + binding + smoke test against a fresh test user
4. Prod migration applied
5. Prod bot row + binding seeded
6. Real smoke test on prod with the principal user
7. `scripts/check_per_bot_panels.py` confirms Rosi shows up in telemetry
8. Final sprint summary

### Phase gate protocol

Phase 1 completes and commits independently. Megaplan posts the phase-1 summary to the conversation log and **stops**. The user explicitly resumes with phase-2 inputs. Do not start phase 2 work before inputs land.

## 4. Sensitivity, safety, and privacy

This bot handles **special-category health data** (GDPR Article 9) and sensitive life events (loss, complications). The following are non-negotiable:

### 4.1 No auto-bridging

The mediator bot's relationship-thread view of the pregnant partner is limited to the one-line gestational summary. Tests must assert:

- No `bridge_candidates` row referencing pregnancy content can be created by any tool
- The dyad hot context render of pregnancy is the hard-coded one-line; never a derived summary of symptoms, weight, or themes
- The `cross_thread_sharing_default` mechanism does NOT apply to pregnancy state (which is health data, not narrative)

If the user explicitly enables broader sharing later (e.g. wants Véas to surface that they had a hard scan day), that's a future Phase 3 conversation — not v1.

### 4.2 No specific medical advice

- The persona MUST always defer specific medical questions to a clinician
- The persona MUST NOT reassure about a red-flag symptom — escalate instead
- The persona MUST NOT give dose/timing advice for medications
- The persona MAY share general well-established information ("most women feel first movements around weeks 18–22") with hedging
- The persona MAY emotionally support, contextualize, and listen — that's the core value-add

Phase 2 critique should focus disproportionately on persona content review. Consider running an independent LLM critique of the persona prompt against an explicit medical-liability checklist before deploying.

### 4.3 Loss handling

The `outcome='loss'` and `outcome='termination'` paths are not edge cases — they happen in real pregnancies, sometimes during the lifetime of an active bot session. The persona MUST:

- Acknowledge the loss directly without euphemism, but with care
- NOT default to celebratory pregnancy framing in the same session
- NOT push "let's track your next pregnancy" or any forward-momentum framing
- Offer presence: "I'm here. What kind of support feels right?"
- Surface clinical resources (miscarriage support groups, bereavement counsellors) when asked but not prescriptively

The 90-day "recent loss/birth" render in hot context exists specifically so the bot maintains awareness across sessions and doesn't accidentally restart cheerful pregnancy chat the next day.

### 4.4 PII and logging

- `pregnancy_*` columns are encrypted only at the cluster level (existing setup) — no per-column encryption added
- Tool call results must NOT echo message content
- Turn audit events log tool names and parameter keys, not values (existing audit pattern)
- Sentry/error logging must NOT include user pregnancy data — wrap any error logs that touch user state in PII-redaction helpers (existing pattern in the codebase; align)

### 4.5 Deletion

A user requesting deletion of pregnancy data: tools should support a `clear_pregnancy()` admin-only path that sets all `pregnancy_*` columns to NULL. Not user-facing in v1; add only the admin path or defer entirely. **Recommend: defer.** Existing user-deletion paths CASCADE cleanly because pregnancy lives on `users`.

## 5. Deployment

### 5.1 Pre-deployment checks

- [ ] Migration 0033 dry-run on prod snapshot: schema valid, constraints fire
- [ ] Migration 0034 dry-run: topic row inserts, no conflict
- [ ] `app/staging.py` includes Tante Rosi staging user — replay tests pass
- [ ] `scripts/check_per_bot_panels.py` runs locally and shows expected bots (will not yet include Rosi until first prod turn)
- [ ] Persona file size — verify total system prompt with persona + hot context stays under per-turn token budget

### 5.2 Deployment order

1. Apply 0033 to staging
2. Apply 0034 to staging
3. Seed `bot_bindings` row for staging test user
4. Smoke test on staging — send a message via the bound transport, verify reply, verify gestational week renders
5. Apply 0033 to prod
6. Apply 0034 to prod
7. Seed prod `bots` row reference + `bot_bindings` row for the principal user
8. Real smoke test on prod
9. Watch logs for 5 minutes — confirm no errors, confirm Rosi shows in `check_per_bot_panels`

### 5.3 Monitoring after deployment

Add to the existing per-bot dashboard (or note absence if dashboard doesn't yet support per-bot view):

- Turn count for `tante_rosi`
- Tool calls — `set_pregnancy_edd` should fire once (onboarding), `correct_pregnancy_edd` rarely, `end_pregnancy` zero in normal use
- OOB triggers — track frequency; spike = red-flag recognition working
- LLM spend — Rosi turns should be in the same cost range as coach turns
- Latency — same expectations as coach

### 5.4 Rollback plan

If persona is rejected post-deploy:
- Bot can be soft-disabled by setting `user_bot_state.paused = true` for affected user(s) — no migration rollback needed
- Persona file is text; revert via git, redeploy
- Schema rollback (0033 down) is only needed if a structural bug surfaces; otherwise leave columns in place

## 6. Success criteria

A user-acceptance test, run on prod with the principal user, that passes all of:

1. **Onboarding** — user tells Rosi they're pregnant; Rosi captures EDD via `set_pregnancy_edd`; next turn the gestational week renders correctly in the prompt
2. **Correction** — user reports a dating scan moved their EDD; Rosi calls `correct_pregnancy_edd`; subsequent renders reflect the corrected EDD
3. **Symptom + escalation** — user reports a red-flag symptom (e.g. "I have a really severe headache and vision is blurry"); Rosi escalates with the clinical-deferral copy AND does not give specific medical advice
4. **Non-red-flag symptom** — user reports a normal symptom (e.g. "I'm exhausted lately"); Rosi acknowledges, contextualizes (with general info + hedging), and offers presence
5. **Mediator side** — Véas, talking to the partner, shows the one-line pregnancy summary in hot context and does NOT auto-bridge any specific pregnancy content
6. **Boundary refusal** — user asks Rosi for relationship advice; Rosi gently redirects to Véas
7. **End-of-arc** *(deferred until end of real pregnancy)* — `end_pregnancy` correctly transitions state; "recent" render appears for 90 days then drops

---

## 7. Deferred items / known follow-ups

- `partner_of` global-2-user assumption (§17 of architecture doc) — Rosi is solo, doesn't trigger it; remains a future cleanup
- 4 pre-existing failing tests (`test_agentic_e2e_ordering_cache_spend_and_oob`, 3× `test_decay.py`) — not blockers
- `decay.py` topic forwarding bug, `scoring.py` missing UUID import — audit-flagged minors, fix opportunistically
- Postpartum-bot sprint design
- Pregnancy history table (for users with multiple pregnancies over time)
- `user_pending_inputs` generalized acquisition mechanism design

---

        Plan:

# Implementation Plan: Tante Rosi — Pregnancy Coach Bot

## Overview
Add a third solo-shape bot (`tante_rosi`) to the existing Veas multi-bot architecture, mirroring the `coach` exemplar (`app/bots/coach.py`) and following `docs/add-new-bot.md`. The brief is well-researched and the architecture (BotSpec, ReadScopes/WriteScopes, TOOL_DISPATCH allowlist, solo vs dyad hot context) is already in place — so this is mostly additive: a migration pair, a pure helper module, three write tools, a BotSpec + persona, two hot-context render hooks, staging seed, and tests. No abstraction over future longitudinal bots; the rejected `docs/longitudinal-state.md` gets a header note only.

Hard internal phase gate: Phase 1 ships schema+plumbing autonomously with a placeholder persona; Phase 2 pauses for user-provided voice samples and red-flag confirmations before persona content lands and prod deploys.

Repo shape observed:
- Bot specs: `app/bots/{base,coach,mediator,registry}.py`. No `app/bots/prompts/` yet.
- Tools: `app/services/tools/{write_tools,read_tools,registry}.py` (write_tools is ~2164 lines; we extend, don't bloat).
- Hot context: `app/services/hot_context_solo.py` (solo, 774 lines) and `app/services/hot_context.py` (dyad, 953 lines).
- Migrations end at `0031_coach_staging_seed.sql` (note: brief says 0033/0034 — see Question Q1).
- Coach is registered lazily under `STAGING=1` to dodge a TOOL_DISPATCH circular import; Rosi must follow the same lazy pattern.

---

## Phase 1: Schema + Plumbing (autonomous)

### Step 1: Audit current bot wiring and prove migration numbering (`app/bots/registry.py`, `migrations/`, `docs/add-new-bot.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Re-read** `docs/add-new-bot.md` end-to-end and `app/bots/coach.py` to confirm the exact pattern (lazy registration in `_maybe_register_staging_bots`, `build_*_spec` factory, allowlist subtraction style).
2. **Confirm** next free migration numbers by `ls migrations/` — current tail is `0031_coach_staging_seed.sql`, so Rosi's pair is **`0032_pregnancy.sql` + `0033_pregnancy_topic.sql`** (the brief's 0033/0034 is off by one; flagged in questions but proceeding with the correct numbers — see assumption A1).
3. **Inspect** `app/services/tools/registry.py` to see `TOOL_DISPATCH` shape and confirm where to add the three new tools.
4. **Annotate** `docs/longitudinal-state.md` with a top-of-file rejection note pointing at this sprint and at the future `docs/pregnancy-state.md` (created in Step 9).

### Step 2: Add pregnancy schema migrations (`migrations/0032_pregnancy.sql`, `migrations/0033_pregnancy_topic.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** `0032_pregnancy.sql` exactly as specified: 8 nullable columns on `mediator.users`, two CHECK constraints (`pregnancy_dating_basis_requires_edd`, `pregnancy_outcome_requires_ended_at`), and the partial index `idx_users_active_pregnancy`.
2. **Write** `0033_pregnancy_topic.sql` inserting the `pregnancy` topic row with `ON CONFLICT (slug) DO NOTHING`.
3. **Add** matching down-files `0032_pregnancy.down.sql` (drops index, constraints, columns) and `0033_pregnancy_topic.down.sql` left empty / commented per the brief's note about cascade safety.
4. **Verify** by applying both against a fresh dev DB via the existing migration runner and `\d mediator.users` / `SELECT * FROM topics WHERE slug='pregnancy'`.

### Step 3: Add the pure helper module (`app/services/pregnancy.py`, `tests/test_pregnancy_helpers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `pregnancy.py` with the five functions in the brief: `gestational_age`, `trimester`, `is_pregnancy_active`, `days_since_loss`, `format_pregnancy_state`. No DB calls; accept user objects (duck-typed: anything with `pregnancy_edd`, `pregnancy_ended_at`, `pregnancy_outcome`, `pregnancy_dating_basis`, optional `timezone`). Use `zoneinfo` for the timezone-aware `today` default; accept an explicit `today` kwarg for tests.
2. **Handle** edge cases per brief: negative gestational age clamps to (0,0) + warning render; EDD > 1y future raises `ValueError`; >2 weeks overdue renders `"42w (overdue, EDD was YYYY-MM-DD)"`; pregnancy older than 90 days post-conclusion returns `None`; `edd` set with `dating_basis=None` logs a warning and returns `None`.
3. **Test** every branch: 0w, 12/13w (trimester boundary), 27/28w, 39w, 40w, 41w, 42w, overdue, recent-loss (<90d), older-loss (>90d), recent-birth, no-pregnancy, data-corruption case, EDD-too-far-future raises.
   ```python
   from datetime import date
   weeks, days = gestational_age(date(2026, 10, 22), today=date(2026, 5, 12))
   assert (weeks, days) == (17, 2)
   ```

### Step 4: Add three pregnancy write tools (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `app/services/tools/registry.py`, `tests/test_pregnancy_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `set_pregnancy_edd`, `correct_pregnancy_edd`, `end_pregnancy` in `write_tools.py` following the existing tool signature pattern (matching `add_memory`/`update_oob`). Each tool:
   - opens a transaction, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on `mediator.users WHERE id = ctx.user.id`
   - validates preconditions (active/no-active pregnancy, valid enum values, EDD within 1y window)
   - applies the update via a single `UPDATE` statement
   - returns `{"ok": True, "edd": "...", "gestational_age": "17w2d"}` with no echoed user content
2. **Register** each tool in `TOOL_DISPATCH` (in `app/services/tools/registry.py`) and add tool schemas to wherever schemas are exported to the LLM (locate by `grep tool_schemas` — current pattern in coach setup). Tool descriptions written in the aunt-voice per brief.
3. **Test** happy paths + precondition errors: already-active on set; no-active on correct/end; invalid `dating_basis`; invalid `outcome`; EDD >1y out; double-`end_pregnancy` error message matches `"pregnancy already ended on <date>"`. Use the existing tools test fixtures (see `tests/test_coach_e2e.py` for the test-pool pattern).

### Step 5: Register the BotSpec with placeholder persona (`app/bots/tante_rosi.py`, `app/bots/registry.py`, `app/bots/prompts/__init__.py`, `app/bots/prompts/tante_rosi.py`, `tests/test_pregnancy_allowlist.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `app/bots/prompts/__init__.py` (new directory) and `app/bots/prompts/tante_rosi.py` exporting `render_system_prompt(**kwargs) -> str`. Phase-1 body is a single line: `"Tante Rosi prompt (phase 2 content pending)."` so the bot is silent-by-design.
2. **Create** `app/bots/tante_rosi.py` with `build_tante_rosi_spec()` modeled on `app/bots/coach.py:build_coach_spec`. Use `participants_shape='solo'`, `primary_topic_slug='pregnancy'`, `read_scopes=ReadScopes(topics=frozenset({'pregnancy'}))` (match coach's narrow scope, not the brief's loose `{"own"}` — see assumption A2), `cross_topic_policy='peek'`, `tool_allowlist = (frozenset(TOOL_DISPATCH.keys()) - COACH_EXCLUSIONS) | {set_pregnancy_edd, correct_pregnancy_edd, end_pregnancy}` where `COACH_EXCLUSIONS` is lifted to a shared constant or duplicated.
3. **Register** Rosi inside `_maybe_register_staging_bots` next to coach, lazily, gated on `STAGING=1`. Add `from app.bots.tante_rosi import build_tante_rosi_spec` only inside the function body.
4. **Test** allowlist invariants in `tests/test_pregnancy_allowlist.py`:
   - `'set_pregnancy_edd' in tante_rosi.tool_allowlist`
   - `'set_pregnancy_edd' not in coach.tool_allowlist`
   - `'set_pregnancy_edd' not in mediator.tool_allowlist`
   - same for the other two pregnancy tools

### Step 6: Wire hot context render — solo path (`app/services/hot_context_solo.py`, `tests/test_pregnancy_hot_context.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Locate** the assembly point in `hot_context_solo.py` (after `topic_status`, before active themes — grep for those section headers).
2. **Insert** a call to `format_pregnancy_state(user)`; if it returns a string, append it; if `None`, omit silently. Gate on `bot_id == 'tante_rosi'` — coach's career bot does NOT render pregnancy state (see assumption A3).
3. **Test** snapshots for: no-pregnancy (section omitted), first-trimester, third-trimester active, overdue, recent loss within 90d, recent birth within 90d, ended >90d ago (section omitted), data-corruption (omitted + warning).

### Step 7: Wire hot context render — dyad path (`app/services/hot_context.py`, tests above)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Locate** the dyad assembly point in `hot_context.py` where partner context is rendered.
2. **Add** a hardcoded one-line `## Partner state` block when `partner.pregnancy_edd IS NOT NULL AND partner.pregnancy_ended_at IS NULL`, OR a recent-loss/birth line when within 90 days. Take both `user` and `partner` as inputs (function already has both); decide off partner.
3. **Assert** in test: dyad render contains ONLY the gestational one-liner — no themes, no observations, no weight, no symptoms. No `bridge_candidates` row gets created from any pregnancy field (assert by inspecting the bridge-candidate code paths or by asserting they never reference pregnancy columns — see Question Q2).

### Step 8: Staging seed + per-bot panels (`app/staging.py`, `scripts/check_per_bot_panels.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a Tante Rosi staging user fixture in `app/staging.py` paralleling the coach staging seed (refer to `migrations/0031_coach_staging_seed.sql` style).
2. **Add** `tante_rosi` to the expected bot list in `scripts/check_per_bot_panels.py` (after deploy; commit so it's tracked).
3. **Create** `evals/per_bot/tante_rosi/.gitkeep`.

### Step 9: Migration test + rejected-doc note (`tests/test_pregnancy_migration.py`, `docs/longitudinal-state.md`, `docs/pregnancy-state.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** `test_pregnancy_migration.py` that applies `0032`+`0033` to a fresh schema and asserts each column, both CHECK constraints (try inserting violating rows), the partial index, and the topic row exist.
2. **Add** a short `docs/pregnancy-state.md` summarizing the per-feature schema choice (mirrors the brief §1 explicit non-goal section).
3. **Prepend** a rejection note to `docs/longitudinal-state.md`: "Rejected 2026-05-12 in favor of per-feature schemas — see `docs/pregnancy-state.md`. Revisit if 3+ bots show pattern overlap."

### Step 10: Phase 1 validation + commit (everything)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest -q tests/test_pregnancy_*.py` first (fastest).
2. **Run** full `pytest -q`; expect the four known pre-existing failures from the brief (1× agentic_e2e, 3× decay) and zero new failures.
3. **Commit** to `rosi-pregnancy-bot`. Phase gate: do NOT push prod migrations, do NOT seed prod bindings, do NOT proceed to Phase 2 persona content.

---

## Phase 2: Persona + Medical Content (requires user inputs — pause)

### Step 11: Solicit user inputs and finalize persona (`app/bots/prompts/tante_rosi.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Pause** and request from user: voice samples (5–10 across registers), red-flag list confirmation/edits, multilingual stance, name confirmation, transport binding (Discord/WhatsApp), optional week-by-week blurbs.
2. **Write** the full persona prompt (80–150 lines) layering: voice register, medical-defer guardrails, loss/grief stance, complication handling, onboarding script for null-EDD, multilingual stance, boundary refusals (deflect relationship-advice to Véas).
3. **Encode** the medical red-flag table (Option A from brief — persona-side recognition) directly in the persona prompt, with the specific symptom→escalation-copy rows from §4.2 plus any user-added entries.
4. **Add** `tests/test_pregnancy_persona.py` asserting key strings present: medical-defer phrase, onboarding script when EDD null, loss-handling phrases when `outcome='loss'`.

### Step 12: OOB recognition test (`tests/test_pregnancy_oob.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Mock** an LLM trace following the pattern used by existing OOB tests (locate via `grep -l oob tests/test_oob_check.py`).
2. **Feed** each red-flag symptom through and assert the response surfaces the escalation copy. Acknowledge this is best-effort given LLM nondeterminism — keep mocked.

### Step 13: Deploy staging then prod (`migrations/`, `bot_bindings`, smoke test)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Apply** `0032` + `0033` to staging; seed staging `bot_bindings`; smoke test via bound transport.
2. **Apply** to prod; seed prod `bots` row + `bot_bindings` for principal user; manual smoke test (Success Criteria §6 in brief).
3. **Watch** logs for 5 min; confirm `tante_rosi` appears in `check_per_bot_panels`.

### Step 14: Final sprint summary (markdown, per brief §8)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Compile** commit list, test delta, files-touched count, LOC delta, phase-1 vs phase-2 cost split, deferred items, known-existing-failures confirmation, prod deploy status.

---

## Execution Order
1. Migrations and helper module first (pure additive, no behavior change).
2. Tools next (depend on schema), then BotSpec (depends on tools for allowlist).
3. Hot context render after BotSpec exists.
4. Staging seed + per-bot panel last in Phase 1.
5. Phase 1 commits and HARD STOPS before persona content.
6. Phase 2 begins only after user provides voice samples + red-flag confirmation.

## Validation Order
1. Helper unit tests (`test_pregnancy_helpers.py`) — fast, pure.
2. Tool tests (`test_pregnancy_tools.py`) — DB-touching but scoped.
3. Allowlist tests (`test_pregnancy_allowlist.py`) — instant.
4. Hot context snapshot tests (`test_pregnancy_hot_context.py`).
5. Migration test (`test_pregnancy_migration.py`).
6. Full `pytest -q` against known baseline.
7. Phase 2: persona tests, OOB tests, staging smoke, prod smoke.

    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 4.5's `_row_matches_topic` helper falls back to global behavior when (table, id) is not in self.artifact_topics \u2014 explicitly for backward compatibility. this is sensible but creates a subtle hazard: a test that forgets to call link_topic for a new artifact sees global decay behavior with no warning, which could mask the very scoping bug the new test in step 12 is meant to catch. the decay scope test does call link_topic for both topics, so it's safe; future authors may not. worth recording but not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-3-join-cutover"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-036",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flag-017 (decay handler backward-compat fallback) is downgraded to accepted maintainability debt with an inline warning comment per step 4.5. the warning text explicitly tells future authors to call link_topic. acceptable, but a noisy assertion (e.g. log a warning when topic_id present in sql but row has no link) would catch silent regressions more loudly. not blocking.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-3-join-cutover"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-037",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11 sql returns last_updated_at, but settopicstatusoutput field is named updated_at. the executor needs to alias on read (e.g., row['last_updated_at']). minor mapping point, not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-auth-topic-status"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 18 coach botspec writes `tool_allowlist=frozenset({...}) - frozenset({'set_topic_status'})` with `{...}` as a placeholder. the executor needs to know what set to start from (probably the union of step_allowed_tools). this is under-specified; the executor will likely just use 'all tools currently in tool_dispatch minus set_topic_status', which is fine, but worth pinning.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-auth-topic-status"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-041",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: botspec.render_system_prompt at app/bots/base.py:63-79 unpacks partner.name and partner.cross_thread_sharing_default positionally before delegating to the renderer. plan step 6 passes partner=none for solo turns and assumption #5 says base.py does not need modification. the **kwargs trick in the solo renderer cannot help \u2014 attributeerror fires inside botspec.render_system_prompt before the renderer runs. plan must commit to patching base.py (or route solo turn around botspec.render_system_prompt).",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-5-solo-bot"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-042",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 6 parameterizes topic_id at agentic.py:753 (event metadata) and 778 (turncontext.primary_topic_id) but omits line 737 where _open_turn(..., topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id(), ...) is hardcoded. without patching line 737, persisted turn rows for coach carry topic_id=relationship \u2014 directly trips stop condition solo bot writes to mediator's relationship topic in any test.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-5-solo-bot"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-047",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: recurring debt (debt-041): are the proposed changes technically correct?: botspec.render_system_prompt unpacks partner.name positionally before delegating; partner=none still breaks regardless of solo renderer **kwargs. plan is unchanged from prior critique. carrying as accepted debt per guidance.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-5-solo-bot"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-048",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: recurring debt (debt-042): are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 6 still omits agentic.py:737 (the _open_turn topic_id kwarg). same gap as prior iteration; trips stop condition if not addressed by executor. carrying as accepted debt.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-5-solo-bot"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-004",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the 0020 migration plan for user_identities omits the verified_at timestamptz column present in architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl, while claiming 'exactly per \u00a73/\u00a712 ddl'. since account-linking workflows are deferred beyond s1, the column is not functionally load-bearing yet, but the claim of exact ddl match is incorrect.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the resolve_binding sql in app/services/routing.py uses limit 1 without order by. if a user ever has both a solo binding and a dyad binding to the same bot, the returned row is non-deterministic. the schema has no unique constraint preventing multiple bindings per (bot_id, user_id). since s1 only seeds one dyad binding and the module is not wired into inbound yet, this is latent future risk rather than an immediate s1 blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: user notes #1/#2 asked to restructure user_actions.md (split t14, move u1 to after execute). the plan addresses the spirit via execution order but does not enumerate user_actions.md content changes. if the harness consumes a separate user_actions.md, it may still reflect pre-revision ordering.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: verified step 8.1 names the scheduler claim query precisely at scheduled_jobs.py:104 (`_claim_due_jobs`). the plan cites returning lines 124-125 but the actual file has the returning list at lines 126-127 (off by ~2 because the plan was computed against a stale snapshot). executor will still find the right block, but the precise line offset is wrong.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-020",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the user note explicitly directs: t2 (eval baseline capture) is blocked by supabase transaction-mode pooler \u2014 set search_path doesn't persist across pooled connections \u2014 and must be deferred, with sprint proceeding on t6\u2013t17. the plan still presents step 2 (eval baseline in d4c2a7c worktree) as the gating phase 0 task with `phase 0 must run first` execution order. plan body and execution order do not reflect the deferral; executor will block on it.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-021",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: validation order steps 1 and 11 still reference the eval baseline (`tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` populated; final `python -m evals run` diff vs baseline). these references should be marked optional / deferred per the user note. currently they read as gates.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-022",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: plan step 17.2 still says 're-run `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1`; diff against tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/'. if t2 is deferred there is no baseline to diff against; this step needs to be conditioned on baseline availability or removed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-030",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: advisory: lint_inserts.py line 325 still contains the comment '# always exit 0 in s2a (advisory).' which will become stale after step 13 makes the linter blocking. the adjacent line 326 todo(s2b) is removed, but this stale advisory comment is not mentioned in the plan. it is cosmetic and will be obvious to the executor when editing line 327, but it is a minor maintainability drift.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-039",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: q1 resolved by stashing tool_schemas.py first (step 21.2) and q4 resolved by numbered 0031 migration with in-sql guard \u2014 but the stash-pop-at-end plan introduces a new risk: sprint edits to tool_schemas.py will conflict with the stashed dirty diff on pop. plan should either drop the stashed tool_schemas.py changes outright or note that conflict resolution is the executor's responsibility.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-auth-topic-status"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-051",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: user note defers t16 (preflight: staging seed + consenting uuid) with explicit instruction to proceed on code-only items 1-8 and treat preflight as pass with todo comments. plan step 9 already gates prod-promotion behind staging verification and falls back to a feedback.md todo \u2014 consistent with the deferral. plan does not however explicitly add 'todo comments in coach scaffold code referencing the operator decision' as the user note requests; executor should add a todo marker in app/bots/coach.py per the note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-5-solo-bot"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: routing unit tests require extending fakepool with `channels`, `user_identities`, `bot_bindings`, `dyads`, and `dyad_members` dicts so the sql patterns in `resolve_bot`, `resolve_sender`, and `resolve_binding` can match against stored rows. however, `tests/conftest.py` is explicitly listed in the brief as a dirty file that must not be touched. the revised plan says \"extend fakepool minimally\" twice (step 9 and step 11 assumption) but never specifies how to do so without modifying `conftest.py`\u2014for example by defining a local `routingfakepool(fakepool)` subclass inside `tests/test_routing.py` or by monkeypatching the class in that test module. if the executor follows the plan literally and edits `conftest.py`, they will violate a locked constraint. if they do not extend fakepool, the routing unit tests cannot be implemented as written. this is a supporting-infrastructure gap.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: step 3 updates scripts/seed_channels.py to import _decode_discord_user_id from the new shared module. the seeder also previously had a regex `_discord_bot_user_id_re` constant (or similar) inline; if the seeder's local regex remains while the new module redefines it, the two could drift. plan should explicitly say the seeder deletes its local copy.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-016",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: plan step 14.3 lists hooks.py emitters but `paused_for_user` does not currently call `logger.*` (verified by reading hooks.py). the plan instructs to log `extra={user_id, bot_id}` \u2014 if there is no existing log call, the executor must add one, which is a behavior change rather than an annotation. minor scope creep.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-023",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py is still listed as a deliverable (step 2.1) and a smoke test (step 2.7 `--help` invocation). if t2 is deferred, the script could either (a) not be written, or (b) be written but its run deferred. plan should specify which. the smoke test alone (`--help`) does not require the pooler.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-024",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 0 contains both step 1 (parity + identity seeding pre-flight) and step 2 (eval baseline). step 1 is independent of the pooler block \u2014 it queries via `psql` or any working connection \u2014 and should not be deferred. plan should make clear step 1 still gates phase 1, but step 2 is parked.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-045",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: get_relationship_topic_id() is referenced in agentic.py at lines 719, 737, 753, 778. step 6 covers 719/721, 753, 767/778, 793 but not 737. same gap as correctness finding \u2014 _open_turn's topic_id kwarg must also be parameterized.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-5-solo-bot"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-052",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recurring debt (debt-045): does the change touch all locations: get_relationship_topic_id() at agentic.py:737 still not in step 6's enumeration. same as debt-042.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-5-solo-bot"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-053",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: plan does not specify that step 5's coach scaffold should carry a todo comment referencing the deferred t16 preflight (operator-held uuid + staging seed verification) per the new user note. minor scope addition the executor must remember.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]

    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan mentions updating app/staging.py:47 'if it calls build_hot_context'. staging.py:47 does call build_hot_context with primary_topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id(). the new allow_cross_topic_peek and allow_cross_topic_status_injection parameters default to false. since staging replay exercises the mediator bot (which has both flags true in its spec), leaving staging.py unchanged means cross-topic peek/status injection is not exercised during staging replay, causing divergence from production behavior. the plan should explicitly state what values to pass.",

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    }
  ],
  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-017",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: step 13.2 adds `bot_id` kwarg to hooks.paused_for_user. callers grep shows it is called from various places; the plan only enumerates messaging.py:170,335 as new bot_id-passing sites. other callers continue to call without bot_id (none default), which is fine, but plan should call out that all existing callers continue to work without modification.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-025",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: evals.runner.run_scenarios is referenced by the deferred step 2.1; plan doesn't enumerate that runner's callers or its schema dependencies. since step 2 is being deferred, this is moot \u2014 but if the script is still authored as a step 2.1 deliverable, it must not import anything that hits the pooler at import time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: plan step 18 strips `bot_spec` defaults from `tests/_scope_helpers.py:make_mediator_ctx`, which has six call sites across `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` (5 calls) and `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` (1 call) that rely on the implicit default. the executor must update all six call sites to pass `bot_spec` explicitly. however, plan step 20's stop-condition nuance lists only steps 14\u201317 as the 'intentional-update list' and omits step 18. if the executor misses a call site and those tests fail, the stop condition text would classify the failure as 'unexpected' and trigger stop, even though the root cause is an incomplete step 18 execution. the stop condition should explicitly include step 18 (or enumerate `test_s2a_stamping.py` and `test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py`) so the executor has permission to fix residual failures in those files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-040",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: step 5.3 read-gate behavior on error path: returns `<outputmodel>(is_error=true, error=err, ...)` with 'populate empty lists'. different read tools have different output shapes (some return single objects e.g. get_theme, get_self_model). plan says 'exact empty-output shape is per-tool' \u2014 but if get_theme returns a non-optional theme dict, the executor needs guidance. minor specificity gap.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-auth-topic-status"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: botspec.render_system_prompt has exactly one caller \u2014 agentic.py:721. step 6 passes partner=none for solo. the base method unpacks partner.name positionally; partner=none breaks the call regardless of solo renderer signature. the single caller passes a value the method cannot currently handle without a base.py edit.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-5-solo-bot"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-060",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the plan updates agentic.py:722-725 (solo path) and 729-732 (dyad path) to pass allow_cross_topic_peek and allow_cross_topic_status_injection flags. however, the staging.py:47 call site is mentioned only as a vague 'if it calls build_hot_context' note. staging replay for mediator should pass allow_cross_topic_peek=true and allow_cross_topic_status_injection=true to mirror production, but the plan does not commit to this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-6-multitopic-hardening"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "issue-hints-completeness": [

  "search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-005",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl for `user_identities` includes a `verified_at timestamptz` column. the plan's 0020 migration ddl omits this column entirely. while account-linking is deferred to s4+, omitting the column now means a future `alter table user_identities add column verified_at` is required, creating unnecessary schema churn. the column can safely be created nullable with no data in s1, matching the architecture doc verbatim.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-006",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the architecture doc \u00a712.1 ddl for `channels` includes `config jsonb not null default '{}'::jsonb`. the plan's 0020 migration ddl omits this column. like `verified_at`, this is a small additive column that should be created now to match the locked schema; deferring it requires a later migration. the executor should add the column to the 0020 ddl.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: step 10 claims `populate_mediator_spec_from_db(pool)` reads 'db-populated fields' for `primary_topic_slug`, `participants_shape`, `read_scopes`, etc. however, the `bots` table ddl in the plan only defines `id`, `display_name`, and `created_at` \u2014 none of the botspec scope fields have db columns. in practice the function will hardcode mediator defaults (`primary_topic_slug='relationship'`, `participants_shape='dyad'`) and only read `display_name` from the db row. the plan's wording could mislead the executor into writing a `select * from bots` expecting columns that do not exist. the implementation should explicitly select only `display_name` and hardcode the rest, or the ddl should be expanded to store bot config. the architecture doc does not specify these columns on `bots`, so hardcoding is correct for s1, but the plan text should be clarified.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the locked brief \u00a716.1 work item 1 explicitly requires 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' seeded inside migration 0020. the revised plan instead moves channel seeding to a runtime script `scripts/seed_channels.py`. while the rationale (env vars differ across environments) is pragmatic, this is a concrete divergence from the locked architecture-doc brief which the plan must consume, not override. if the executor applies only the sql migrations, the `channels` table will be empty and routing will have no rows to resolve, breaking the mid-sprint checkpoint 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' gate. the runtime script must be documented as a mandatory post-migration step, but the brief expected it in the migration itself.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]

    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-002",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-011",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-019",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [

        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-026",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-031",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2b-constraints"
        ]
      },

        "id": "DEBT-034",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 4.5's `_row_matches_topic` helper falls back to global behavior when (table, id) is not in self.artifact_topics \u2014 explicitly for backward compatibility. this is sensible but creates a subtle hazard: a test that forgets to call link_topic for a new artifact sees global decay behavior with no warning, which could mask the very scoping bug the new test in step 12 is meant to catch. the decay scope test does call link_topic for both topics, so it's safe; future authors may not. worth recording but not a blocker.",

        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",

1	"""Solo 'coach' bot profile (career topic).
2	
3	S4 pre-flight only: BotSpec wired but the prompt renderer + hot context code
4	live in S5. The spec is registered in BOT_SPECS only when STAGING env truthy.
5	
6	TODO (T16 deferred): staging seed verification and consenting prod user UUID
7	are operator-held; prod-promotion migration is gated on these per locked
8	decision #6 — see feedback.md.
9	"""
10	
11	from __future__ import annotations
12	
13	from typing import Any
14	from uuid import UUID
15	
16	from app.bots.base import BotSpec, ReadScopes, WriteScopes
17	from app.services.prompts_solo import render_solo_system_prompt
18	
19	
20	def _coach_prompt_renderer(
21	    assistant_name: str,
22	    user_name: str,
23	    partner_name: str | None = None,
24	    *,
25	    prompt_version: str = "v1",
26	    onboarding_state: str | None = None,
27	    current_user_sharing_default: str | None = None,
28	    partner_sharing_default: str | None = None,
29	    **kwargs: Any,
30	) -> str:
31	    """Coach prompt renderer — delegates to the solo system prompt.
32	
33	    Accepts partner_name, partner_sharing_default, and partner (via
34	    **kwargs) from BotSpec.render_system_prompt but ignores them.  The
35	    solo renderer has no dyadic concepts.
36	    """
37	    return render_solo_system_prompt(
38	        assistant_name,
39	        user_name,
40	        prompt_version=prompt_version,
41	        onboarding_state=onboarding_state,
42	        sharing_default=current_user_sharing_default,
43	        topic_display_name="career",
44	    )
45	
46	
47	_MIN_STEP_INSTRUCTIONS = {
48	    "read": "Read step (coach S4 stub).",
49	    "consult": "Consult step (coach S4 stub).",
50	    "respond": "Respond step (coach S4 stub).",
51	    "record": "Record step (coach S4 stub).",
52	    "schedule": "Schedule step (coach S4 stub).",
53	    "done": "Done step (coach S4 stub).",
54	}
55	
56	
57	def build_coach_spec() -> BotSpec:
58	    """Build the coach BotSpec.
59	
60	    Lazy import of TOOL_DISPATCH avoids a circular import (registry imports
61	    write_tools, which doesn't import coach).  The tool_allowlist is the
62	    full dispatch table minus dyad-only tools (bridge candidates, partner
63	    escalation, and set_topic_status — per §16.6 locked decisions).
64	    """
65	    from app.services.tools.registry import TOOL_DISPATCH
66	
67	    return BotSpec(
68	        bot_id="coach",
69	        prompt_renderer=_coach_prompt_renderer,

15	_RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID: UUID | None = None

17	BOT_SPECS: dict[str, BotSpec] = {

23	
24	def _maybe_register_staging_bots() -> None:
25	    """Register staging-only bots (coach) lazily.
26	
27	    Called from get_bot_spec on first access. Lazy registration avoids a
28	    circular import: coach.build_coach_spec() pulls TOOL_DISPATCH which
29	    transitively imports messaging/hooks/app.bots.registry — so doing it at
30	    module-import time deadlocks under STAGING=1.
31	    """
32	    global _STAGING_BOTS_REGISTERED
33	    if _STAGING_BOTS_REGISTERED:
34	        return
35	    _STAGING_BOTS_REGISTERED = True
36	    if os.environ.get("STAGING", "").lower() in {"1", "true", "yes"}:
37	        from app.bots.coach import build_coach_spec
38	
39	        coach = build_coach_spec()
40	        BOT_SPECS[coach.bot_id] = coach
41	
42	
43	class UnknownBotSpec(ValueError):
44	    pass
45	
46	
47	def get_bot_spec(bot_id: str) -> BotSpec:
48	    _maybe_register_staging_bots()
49	    try:
50	        return BOT_SPECS[bot_id]
51	    except KeyError as exc:
52	        known = ", ".join(sorted(BOT_SPECS))
53	        raise UnknownBotSpec(f"unknown bot spec: {bot_id}; known specs: {known}") from exc
54
